Factory Reset (if available)
The Factory Reset function allows users to restore the vehicle system to its original state and delete all stored personal data. This feature is provided through the central infotainment display and can only be performed by the Primary profile.
To perform a factory reset:
On the Profile Management (All Profiles) screen, tap the three-dot icon in the top-right corner to open options.
Select “Factory Reset”.
Confirm and continue.
Enter the PIN code to start the reset process.
NOTE
Once the process is complete, the fullscreen loading screen will be dismissed and the system will return to the normal MHU interface. If the factory reset is successful, the system will proceed to the initial setup (first-time onboarding). If it fails, the Primary profile will remain and a message “Factory reset failed” will be displayed.
The gear cannot be shifted during the installation process.
The vehicle will not perform a factory reset if:
The gear is not in Park (P).
The handbrake is not applied.
The vehicle has not been started.
The low-voltage battery is below 20%.
The low-voltage battery’s voltage is below 11.5V.
An emergency call is in progress.
Awaiting an emergency call back.

## Navigation (HNAV)

Navigation Overview (HNAV)
Use Navigation App to navigate to a selected destination. Route information is displayed on the Infotainment screen and Head Up Display (if equipped).
To open Navigation App:
Tap  App Library    on the Infotainment screen.
Tap Maps.
OR
Tap  Navigation widget  on the Home Screen.
Navigation Page Overview
Search for a Destination
Point Of Interest
Map layer
Isoline routing
Traffic incident
Navigation Settings
Navigation Safety
Before using the Navigation System carefully read and understand the following warning.
WARNING
Be aware of all local traffic laws and road characteristics of your region. It is always your responsibility to determine the safety of the route being suggested. Drive the vehicle appropriately according to current road and environmental conditions.
Always follow road traffic rules and regulations even when driving with the assistance of the Navigation System. The Navigation system is not a substitute for safe driving.
One-box Search
The user can search for a destination by inputting general categories and terms, specific place names or geocoordinates into the search bar.
To search for a destination:
Tap on  Search  bar    .
Enter Name of the destination.
Tap on  Search.
NOTE
When user starts typing some of the common categories such as Restaurants, Charging, Parking, the map will suggest a shortcut to the category search.
WARNING
Do not use Search feature while vehicle is in motion, as it can cause distraction and lead to accidents causing serious injuries or death.
Search by Category
Destinations can be searched by category.
To search by Category/Point Of Interest (POI):
Tap  Search    icon.
Select desired  Category  from the available options.
Place Detail Card
The Place Detail Card will be displayed on the Infotainment screen after selecting a destination. The Place Detail Card shows detailed information about the selected destination including (when available).
NOTE
You can also make a call by tapping on phone number displayed on the Place Detail Card.
Spatial Filters
The spatial filters are available for both category search and free text search when guidance is active. The filters are shown as buttons on top of the search results list.
After selecting a destination, user can continue to search for an additional destination with spatial filters: Along the route, At destination and Nearby.
Recent destinations
When user accesses the Search bar, it automatically displays a list of recent search queries and Point of Interest (POI) for easier selection.
The recent destination items are clickable and provide a shortcut directly to the place detail card, where the user can immediately start navigation, request route alternatives to the location or add the location as an additional waypoint to an already existing route. Users can clear the entire search history and delete items from the search history list.
Saved Places
Places can be saved as Favorites, Home or Work.
To save a Place:
Tap  Save    icon on the Place Detail Card to save a place as Favorite.
Select an option from the list to save/set a place as  Favorite/Home/ Work.
To remove Saved Places:
Tap  Saved    icon on the Place Detail Card
Tap  Remove from favorites
NOTE
Only one place can be saved as Home/Work. Also, same place cannot be saved in both Home and Work category, at the same time.
The Navigation App requires a cellular connection for some functionality. Local carrier fees will apply.
Destination entry from the map
User can select any point on the map, including POIs, and access further available information about a given location including address and geocoordinates, as well nearby POIs.
When in exploration mode, the opened location can be set as a destination. When in guidance mode, the opened location can either be set as an additional waypoint or a new destination.
To see more information about a place, search for available routes, or start guidance, tap on a place marker.
NOTE
When user selects a point on the map, outside the road network or POI locations, the address for that location will be based on the closest road network.
Offline map
Users are able to download the map data to use when there is no connection to the internet.
To use offline map:
Go to  Settings > Maps > Offline maps
Select a country name to download offline map for that country
NOTE
It is recommended that users download their maps before embarking on a guided route.
When offline, map does not present POI imagery or ratings information.
Offline search is significantly slower than online search.
Traffic
Online traffic is supported and traffic information is displayed on the map.
Traffic flows on the map
Traffic flow is displayed on the map for different road classes depending on the zoom level. Flow for road closures and road works are shown on the map depending on the zoom level.
Traffic on route
The traffic colors on the route is synchronizes with the traffic as seen on the map.
This feature combines the live traffic data with the traffic info that is already contained within the routing response for the rest of the route. The routing response contains some future projections using historic traffic patterns.
Traffic incidents on the map
Traffic incidents are displayed in the map viewport depending on the zoom level. Icons for traffic incidents are rendered as map markers as an overlay to the traffic flow in the 2D and 3D map views. Traffic incidents are tappable and enlarge when selected by user. Traffic incident details are provided on an incident details card when a user taps on them within the application UI, including:
Title
Traffic event text (description) with all supported languages
End time, available for some types of traffic incidents
List view of traffic incidents
Users can open the list view of traffic incidents via a dedicated button in the application UI.
In the exploration view, traffic incidents within a radius around the current vehicle position are shown. Users can select and see more information about a given incident by tapping on the incident in the list.
When the traffic list feature is opened during an active guidance session, users can modify the spatial filter to also see traffic incidents along the route or around the current car position.
In the traffic incident detail card, users can see the estimated end date for traffic disruption for two types of incidents: road closures and road construction.
Traffic incident alert
Users can turn on and off notifications for traffic incidents from the settings.
To turn on/off traffic incident alert:
Go to  Settings > Road alerts > Traffic incidents
Toggle of  Show notifications  to turn on/off.
Alerts are displayed and sounded 1000 meters before the incident locations. These alerts are visible for 6 seconds unless a user taps on the details or dismisses the alert. Once the time has elapsed, the notification disappears.
NOTE
Traffic incidents feature is OFF by default.
This feature works online only.
Routing
Turn-by-turn, maneuver-based visual and voice guidance instructions are provided to allow the driver to easily follow the planned route.
Visual Guidance
Drivers see the active route along with the traffic information, points of interest, incidents, and other relevant information.
The upcoming maneuver is always displayed in the Turn-By-Turn (TBT) widget during navigation. The next maneuver also is displayed, when it follows soon after the upcoming maneuver (500m).
The following maneuver display is replaced by Lane Guidance instructions where applicable.
Voice Guidance (If available)
Upcoming maneuver is verbally announced with a street name according to the chosen languages.
To unmute/mute voice guidance:
Go to  Settings > Audio > Audio Guidance
Select  Audio guidance  to turn on Audio Guidance
OR
Select  No audio guidance  to turn off Audio Guidance.
Dynamic rerouting
When in routing or guidance mode, the fastest route can be suggested to the driver, if possible.
If the user selected the shortest possible route, other routes will not be suggested.
If there are no alternatives and the route overview is open, the alternate routes are not shown. If there are some options available,up to two alternatives in the route overview card will be displayed and rendered on map. If the route overview card is closed, for example during guidance, drivers only see the alternatives that are faster than the currently active route.
For routes with auto-added chargers, alternatives that have the same or a longer travel time during guidance are filtered out. The chargers are shown on the routes as white dots.
To turn on alternate route alerts:
Go to  Settings > Route options
Toggle the  Get alerts for alternate routes  on
NOTE
Routing alternatives work online and offline.
The offline option does not have the most current data (such as traffic flows or incidents), so the estimated time arrival is calculated based on distance and average road conditions.
A notification in guidance mode is displayed when there is a faster route available. After a user accepts the alternative, the application automatically updates the route.
Lane Assistance with Voice Instructions
Users see the upcoming lanes (lane markings and lane dividers) in the Turn-by-turn card. The optimal lane to take is highlighted for the user during guidance to their destination. An audible lane assistance based on the lane information during guidance to the destination is provided to the user with announcement timing aligned to functional road classes.﻿﻿
Color-coded Estimated Time of Arrival (ETA)
The estimated time of arrival (ETA) is color coded based on live traffic. When a driver starts guidance to any location, the ETA color immediately reflects the current traffic level on the entirety of a chosen route, or a route saved to the commute assistant.
ETA color codes reflect how heavy the traffic is at the current time at any point of the route. The meaning of colors are defined as follows:
Green : no traffic on the route.
Yellow : Medium traffic on the route.
Red : Heaviest traffic levels.
NOTE
This feature works online. The ETA traffic level color updates after each route refresh.
Speed Alerts (if available)
During guidance and nonguidance, the application displays the current travel speed and the legally allowed speed limit information with their given units (kmph or mph) for the current road.
Speed limit signage is displayed where the information is available according to locally prevailing standards. When the information is not available, the signage is shown as generic signs.
Driver is warned visually and audibly when exceeding the current speed limit. Driver can set a threshold in 5 kmph/3 mph steps above the legal speed limit above which warnings are issued.
NOTE
Speed limit information and warnings are switched ON by default.
This feature works online and offline, since vehicle speed data does not depend on the network availability.
Safety Alerts (if available)
Safety cameras are divided into categories such as speed cameras and red-light cameras.
To turn on/off safety cameras display on the map:
Go to  Settings > Navigation: Speed > Safety Alerts
Toggle the  Show on map  on/off
To turn on/off safety cameras alerts:
Go to  Settings > Speed > Safety Alerts
Toggle the  Alert when approaching  on/off
To turn on/off safety cameras sound alerts:
Go to  Settings > Speed > Safety Alerts
Toggle the  Sound alert in vicinity  on/off
When active, the safety cameras alerts are announced during guidance with a first warning 500 meters before the position of the safety cam when driving below 80 km/h, and 800 meters before the safety camera when driving above 80 km/h. A pulsating camera icon highlights the relevant camera on the map. A final warning is provided 100 meters before the actual camera position.
Upcoming safety cameras are announced to the driver as an audio prompt. In the exploration view (guidance switched off), all cameras are displayed on the map when the vehicle is idle (below 5 km/h). When the vehicle is moving (speed is 5 km/h or more), the number of speed cameras displayed on the map depends on the chosen zoom level and the current vehicle position to avoid a cluttered map experience.
The driving direction affected by the speed camera will be highlighted with an arrow emerging from the safety camera it is in proximity of the current vehicle position, and an audio alert will be provided analogously to the implementation in the guidance context.
School Zones
A school zone refers to an area on a street near a school or near a crosswalk leading to a school that may have younger pedestrians present. School zones generally have a reduced speed limit during certain hours.
When a driver approaches a school zone, a series of audible pings will be sounded as a warning and a warning icon on the screen will be displayed with a distance countdown in the selected measurement units. When entering and leaving the school zone, the map announces and displays the school zone icon on the screen.
To enable this feature, select Settings > Navigation: Road alerts and enable School zone alerts. Times when to display the alert are configurable, so a different time can be set for every day of the week (either by users or OEMs).
To enable/disable school zone alerts display
Go to  Settings > Navigation > Road alerts > School zone alerts
Toggle the  Show alerts for school zones  on/off
To enable/disable school zone alerts sound:
Go to  Settings > Navigation > Road alerts > School zone alerts
Toggle the  Hear alerts for school zones  on/off
NOTE
This feature works online and offline.
This feature is location-based and only available in the US and Canada.
Intersection Map Display (ISM)
When vehicle approaches an intersection and navigation is active, the map displays a 2D visual representation showing the relevant maneuver. A distance bar provides a visual indication of the distance and time remaining to the maneuver point.
When in range of the intersection, the current vehicle location appears in the ISM widget.
To enable/disable intersection view
Go to  Settings > Navigation > Guidance > Intersection view
Toggle the  Show junction view  on/off
Junction View
When a vehicle approaches a junction, the map displays a 2D generic visual representation of a junction view in a widget under the next maneuver card. The widget shows all the lanes in the direction of travel and marks the lanes that are recommended for continuing along the selected route with an arrow.
The 2D junction view safely and precisely guides drivers to the lane they need to select with all the most necessary information being highlighted. The information is displayed in a widget that pictures how the lanes divide or merge and which ones to choose to stay on the selected route to allow drivers to prepare in time even before they enter the junction.
To enable/disable junction view:
Go to  Settings > Navigation > Guidance > Junction view
Toggle the  Show junction view  on/off
NOTE
Generally, this feature works online. However, if user has downloaded a map for the relevant region, then the junction view is available offline as well. If there is no junction view data or if the guidance is in offline mode, the map always fall back to the standard intersection map display (ISM).
This feature is OFF by default.
Parking Approach Mode
When approaching the final destination, user will be notified with parking opportunities.
The map first displays the single, most relevant parking opportunity with an option to navigate there easily. Driver can also search for parking alternatives by tapping on the alternatives button. Once driver selects a parking place, the map begins guidance to the parking place without discarding the original destination. A dotted line on the map from the chosen parking POI to the destination visualizes the pedestrian route the driver can follow. .
To enable/disable parking approach mode:
Go to  Settings > Navigation > Parking > Parking guidance
Toggle the  Suggest parking when close to destination  on/off
NOTE
The parking approach mode only offers static parking information including parking address, opening hours, and telephone number.
Navigation Guidance View
Navigation Guidance View and turn by turn navigation information is displayed on the Infotainment screen once a navigation route is selected. If another native App is opened, the turn by turn navigation information is displayed on Head Up Display (if equipped).
Guidance Card
Add a stop
Arrival time, Battery level
End Route
To add a stop in the route:
Tap  Add stop   .
Select a stop.
To remove a stop:
Tap  Remove Stop .
EV Features
The EV features are well integrated into the overall guidance experience and can make use of charge levels and other (EV) specific parameters of the vehicle. Most EV features are available only when the vehicle is online.
Charging Stations with Dynamic In﻿formation
You can find and add a charging station as a destination using Destination Search.﻿ See  > Navigation > Destination Search.
The map offers spatial filters to search for EV POIs when guidance is active: around the current car position, at the destination, along the route.
Users are shown the EV POI icons based on three states (where is data available and shared by charge point operator):
Charge port is available.
No charge port is available (including “broken” and “in maintenance”)
Only static data is available/status unknown
Availability information for charging stations is also shown to the user within the search results list (where data is available).
Once a user selects an EV charging station from the results list by clicking on the map marker, the map displays more granular data including availability, charging speeds, charge cards, and available plug type ports.
User can sort EV charging stations based on availability and distance information.
User can filter EV POI category search results by charge speed and charging point operator.
To set a charging speed preference:
Go to EV Charging > Charging Speed .
Select Any/Slow/Fast/Super fast only
To select plug types preference:
Go to EV Charging > Connector Type
Toggle on/off CS1/CS2/Tesla/CHAdeMO/Type1/Type2
To select charging setup time:
Go to EV Charging > Comfort zone > Setup time
Select 2 minutes/ 5 minutes/ 10 minutes
NOTE
The charging setup time refers to the duration spent after arriving at a charging station but before the actual charging begins.
The setup time is considered in the overall calculation of charging time at a station to ensure accurate route planning and energy management for electric vehicles.
Charge Cards (if available)
The supported eMobility Service Providers (eMSP) is displayed. When drivers have a charge card, they want to see EV charging points appropriate for that particular card and to have routing and guidance prioritize charging points that have the selected charging cards.
The charge point search result list and details card include the information on whether a charging point supports a charge card in addition to the other options. Users can add and remove charging cards.
To select preferred charge card:
Go to  EV Charging > Charging Cards > Add cards .
Add/remove your EV charging cards
NOTE
When in routing or guidance mode, the selected charging cards is prioritised whenever possible. The place details card for a charging station indicates whether the charging point accepts the selected card.
eMSP charging card logo
The eMSP charging card logo is displayed in the charging card settings, near the charge point marker on the map during guidance and routing, and in search results, allowing drivers to recognize preferred service providers.
Apart from the current availability status of charging stations (green for available, red for occupied, and grey for those lacking data), drivers can also see at a glance which charging cards are accepted.
NOTE
This feature works online and offline ; however, the offline option might not have the most current data.
Isoline routing
The navigation offers a dedicated map view button to access the range map view.
The remaining range on map is presented to the user as a polygon on the map depicting how far they can travel from the current car position considering current State of Charge (SoC) of the vehicle’s battery.
The range map shows the user-defined comfort zone from the application settings in a different color.
Multi-stop Route Planning
When planning a route while driving an EV, the navigation automatically suggests automatically adding charging points, according to the SoC and charge point preferences set by the user.
When the navigation identifies the destination cannot be reached with the current battery SoC, it asks the user if it should take over charge planning.
Users can access the route preference via the route plan and switch multi-stop routing on or off at any point in a journey. Handover between both modes is seamless and the route is recalculated after a setting adjustment.
A multi-stop route is calculated based on the current SoC of the vehicle’s battery and the vehicle-specific consumption model.
With a multi-stop route is calculated, the charging point preferences set by the user, such as charging speed, are taken into account. If preferred charge cards are set, the navigation prioritizes charging points that accept the chosen charge cards, whenever possible on the route.
Low Battery Alert
Drivers receive a low battery alert when SOC of vehicle is below 20%
The low battery alert initially shows the user the nearest charging station to the Current Car Position (CCP).
Users can choose to drive to the nearest charging station or look for additional charging stations around the current car position.
To turn on/off low power alert:
Go to  EV Charging > Range Support
Toggle on/off  Low power alert
Auto-added Charge Points
In multi-stop route planning mode, charging stops are added to the route automatically by the routing service, if the driver does not opt out of this option.
The user-defined minimum charge expectation (“comfort zone”) when reaching a destination, waypoint, or a charge stop as defined by the user in the navigation settings is also taken into account.
The user-defined maximum level of charging at a charging point is also included in the route calculation.
The user is shown the route in the HMI with information such as the number of charging stops, charging time, expected charge at arrival, and charge level to be reached at the charging stop, and the predicted energy consumption for the given route(s).
During guidance, the Dynamic Route Guidance (DRG) feature also displays route alternatives with charging time differences among the different routes presented.
If no charging station can be found in multi-stop routing, users are shown the range on the route instead.
To turn on/off low power alert:
Go to  Settings > Route options > Routing
Toggle on/off  Add Charging Stops
NOTE
This feature works online only.
This feature is ON by default.
When the auto-add toggle is switched off, the charging points are removed from the route.
If there is enough battery to reach the set destination, no charging stations are added to the route.
If the auto-add charging option is deactivated; for example, by changing the route options, all automatically added charging points are also removed.
If auto-add charging is activated, the manually added charging stations are removed.
When approaching an auto-added charger at the end of a journey (last charge point within a 2 kilometer radius around a set destination) before arriving at destination, drivers have the option to deactivate auto-added charging and to ignore the last charge stop on their route.
Manual Charge Stop Planning
When the auto-add charging stops feature is deactivated, the navigation still allows the driver to see the battery’s SoC along the route with regard to the set comfort zone, and see how far the vehicle can be driven with the current battery level.
When in the manual EV routing mode, the navigation recognizes manually added charging points and updates the route range accordingly.
When driving, drivers may have a preference for specific charging points.
When a charge stop is added manually, the navigation internally recognizes it as a charge stop. When multi-stop routing is deactivated, the navigation assumes that the vehicle is always charged to the level defined in the comfort zone settings.
The EV range information is automatically updated and presented to the driver when the charging stop has been added to the route. The comfort zone is displayed on the route during route planning and during the route overview.
The reachable distance is visible on the map in the standard route color (blue). The part of the route that is outside of the range of the EV is marked in red.
The navigation recommends the charge level needed to complete the route (with the comfort zone percentage taken into account) as it does for all charge points.
To add a manual charging point, search for charging points either along the route or by using the search box.
In the details card of the charging point, select  Add stop  to add it to the route. the navigation automatically recognizes the charging stop and recalculates the route with regard to the battery level.
NOTE
Manual charge stop planning only works when auto-add charging stops feature is deactivated.
Map & Display
2D/3D/Satellite map views
The navigation supports a 3D-map view and a rich set of visualizations on the map, including a set of automotive-grade buttons, which enable users to easily configure their preferred map view.
Default map view shows the current position of the user according to the vehicle location.
Satellite and default map view:
The navigation provides a default map view and a satellite view with a street network and map Point of Interest (POI) information overlay..
NOTE
Any map layer type can be used in both guidance and non-guidance scenarios.
Map layer
The following map layer choices are supported by the navigation:
Default geographic map view.
Satellite map.
POI layers
Points of interest (POIs) are displayed as clickable icons on the map.
To select Point of Interest categories:
Go to  Settings > Maps > Map display > Places on map
Select POI categories to show on map
NOTE
The selected POI category groups persist in guidance and non-guidance scenarios.
3D Buildings
3D landmarks (where available) are rendered with realistic full-color textures.
To select 3D appearance:
Go to  Settings > Navigation > Guidance > Buildings and Landmarks
Select  Show buildings and landmarks 3D/ Show only landmarks/ Show none.
Compass
You can find and add a charging station as a destination using Destination Search.﻿ See > Navigation > Destination Search.
Smart caching & streaming
Map streaming with smart caching:
The navigation automatically downloads and caches map tile bundles from backend based on usage such as the current car position, route request, or map panning by a user. Route-related map data is pre-cached within a corridor around the route with a radius of 10km.
Catalog map download:
Catalog map download enables users to download complete country maps Over The Air (OTA). While the map downloads are updated yearly, per standard licensing agreement, the update process itself requires user acceptance. Catalog items are divided by administrative area (for example, country, state within a country, country-level regions, where applicable) and can be downloaded when users click on the catalog item.
The map is stored as a protected map on the vehicle. The download does not expire, unless a general update of the map access manager is required or the HERE Navigation subscription ends. HERE Navigation has an optimized hand-over logic for operating from a map stream or cache while online and from the protected on-board map when offline.
NOTE
The map stream and the map data download of a protected map are mandatory sourcing components to use HERE Navigation. HERE Navigation and the HERE location platform, from which content and connected services are delivered, are fully integrated. Map streaming and caching and on-board map downloads and integration with search, routing, and traffic services all work off-the-shelf.

# Battery & Charging


## High Voltage Components

High Voltage Components Overview
All of these components use, control and/or supply High Voltage Electricity:
Front Electric Drive System (EDS)
HV Compressor
HV Heater
Power Distribution Unit, On Board Charging, DC-DC Converter
Vehicle Control Unit (VCU)
HV Battery
Charge Port
HV Wiring
Rear Electric Drive System (EDS)
EMI Filter
WARNING
In the unlikely event of a vehicle fire, immediately contact the local emergency responders.
The High Voltage system has no user serviceable parts. Do not remove or replace high voltage components, cables or connectors.
High Voltage cables are typically colored orange for easy identification.
Contact with High Voltage can lead to fire, electric shock, serious injury or danger to life.
Read and follow all instructions on the labels that are attached to the vehicle. These are provided for your safety.
Even when the battery is low or discharged, High Voltage can still be present throughout the vehicle’s electrical system.

## Charging Instructions

Charging Equipment
The vehicle can be charged via a number of different charging devices. These include Mobile Chargers, Charging Stations, Super Fast Charging Stations and Home Chargers.﻿ The charging devices must have the correct charging adapter to plug into the vehicle.
Vehicle Inlet Socket
This vehicle supports the Combined Charging System (CCS). Depending on the vehicle variant, either the CCS1 or CCS2 standard is provided.
CCS 1 charging handle
CCS 2 charging handle
NOTE
If your vehicle is equipped with a charging inlet that differs from the plug type of the available charger, a adapter such as a CCS1 to CCS2 converter (or vice versa) is required. To ensure safety, always use a genuine adapter. Please contact VinFast for detailed information and assistance in selecting the appropriate adapter.
WARNING
When using an adapter, the vehicle cannot lock the charging handle. Therefore, never remove the charging handle during charging to ensure safety.
Mobile Charger (Level I 220V charger)
If available, mobile charger provides slow charging of the vehicle via AC electricity.
To use mobile charger:
Plug the mobile charger into the 220V power supply socket.
Plug the charging handle into the vehicles charging port.
The vehicle will start to charge via Mobile Charger.
CAUTION
The mobile charger will not work without a ground connection. The wall socket requires a ground pin to connect to the mobile charger. This is required to safely ground the charging system and avoid short circuits.
If the desired wall socket does not have a ground connection, please contact a licensed electrician to have this rectified.
Charging Stations
The vehicle can also be charged at public charging stations. Charging stations may provide either AC or DC charging at various charging rates. Due to the variability between charging stations it is critical to familiarize yourself with the operating instructions of each Charging Station correctly to prevent possible damage to the vehicle and to ensure safety.
Charge Station Identification
To ensure that only a compatible charging station is connected to your vehicle the charge port includes an identification label identifying both the vehicle charging handle type and permissible charging voltage and current limits. Both the vehicle’s AC and DC charge connector type will be represented on this label by a charge type identifier as shown below.
Charge Connector Type
Charge Type Identifier
AC Charging
(Type 2)
DC Charging
(CCS2)
OR
For more detail in regards to charge status indicator lights, See >   Charging Instructions.
WARNING
Always ensure the Charge Type Identifier of both the vehicle and charge station match for either AC or DC charging. Failure to use a suitable charge station may lead to damage to the vehicles high voltage battery or charging system.
NOTE
The use of Super Fast Charging Stations should be minimized to ensure the optimal lifetime performance of the battery.
Home Charger
A home charger can be installed at home or office to provide convenient charging. Typically these will charge the vehicle quicker than the Mobile Charger.
Home chargers need to be hard wired to the power supply in the home. Please contact a licensed electrician for installation.
Please contact a VinFast dealership for information on suitable VinFast wall-mounted home chargers.
Important Safety Warnings regarding Charging
WARNING
Contact with a High Voltage power source can lead to serious injury or risk to life.
Do not touch the contacts on the charging equipment or in the charging port of the vehicle.
Never use damaged, faulty or modified charging equipment.
Never use an extension cord, adapter or power board with charging equipment.
Protect the charging socket and contacts from water or moisture and other liquids.
The charger can generate electromagnetic waves that can affect medical devices such as an implantable cardiac pacemaker.
Always confirm with the medical team and manufacturer whether charging the electric vehicle will impact the operation of the medical devices such as a cardiac pacemaker.
There should be no water or dust in the charging handle connector and plug. Always make sure they are clean before connecting to the charger and charging inlet. Failure to do so may cause electric shock when connection the charging handle or even lead to a fire.
Be careful not to touch or insert anything in the charging outlet holes, charging port holes, and the charging handle holes when connecting the charging handle to the charging port in the vehicle.
Immediately stop charging if abnormal symptoms are detected such as odor or smoke.
If the charge cable outer conduit is damaged then immediately replace the charging cable to prevent electrical shock.
Charging Instructions
Electric vehicles are powered by batteries and motors which use electrical energy that is stored in a high voltage battery.﻿ The high voltage battery can be charged by any of the following methods:
Level I:  The vehicle is supplied with a Level 1 charging cable. The vehicle can be charged from grounded household sockets using Level 1 cables. A household socket connection charges with alternating current. In the case of Level 1 charging cables, the efficiency values may differ from those on the energy label.
Level II:  Using a special plug, a Level 2 charging cable allows users to quickly charge at designated Level 2 charging stations. The charging at Level 2 stations is performed using alternating current. Charging can be completed much faster at a charging station rather than at a household socket.
Level III:  DC charging stations are equipped with a permanent DC charging cable, which makes charging possible. Charging is carried out at DC charging stations using direct current. A DC charger station with a higher current connection charges more quickly than a household socket or a Level 2 charging station.
NOTE
The time it takes to charge the vehicle will depend on the ambient air and battery temperature, the power level of the charger, the charge level of the battery, and the charger type.
The speed of Level III DC Super Fast Charging may be reduced in some circumstances, particularly when the ambient temperature is high. Hence charging will take more time.
WARNING
Chargers generate electromagnetic waves that can seriously affect medical electrical devices, such as implantable cardiac pacemakers. Consult your medical professional or pacemaker manufacturer to ensure the charging of your electric vehicle will not impact the operation of your medical electrical devices.
Never forcefully pull the charging handle from the charging port. Dangerous electrical arcs could result, causing serious injury or death. Always follow the charging shut down process of the charging device and vehicle.
To charge the vehicle follow the process below and the instructions of the charging device. Observe all Warnings and Cautions.
Put the gear into Park and apply the Park Brake.
Check the charging device is compatible with the vehicle.
Open the Charge Port Door and remove the protective caps.
Fully insert the charging handle into the charge port, ensuring it is connected correctly.
Start the charging process at the charging device.
The charging handle will be automatically locked in the charging port during charging.
Observe the charging status on the charge port light, the vehicle display and the charging device.
Lock the vehicle.
To stop charging, unlock the vehicle and follow the instructions on the charging device and on the vehicle display.
The locking pin in the charge port will automatically disengage once charging has stopped. This may take some time after charging has been requested to stop.
Remove the charging handle from the charge port and return it to the charging device.
Replace the charging port caps and close the charging port door.
Further instructions and important information on charging can be found in this section.
Charging Port
The charge port is located at the driver`s side front fender and locked by the central locking system. Open the charge door to access the charge port.
To open the charge port door﻿:
To open the charge port door it first must be unlocked and the vehicle must be in Park (P). You may unlock the charge door by:
Tapping the Unlock button in the  EV App  on the touchscreen.﻿
Using  Emergency Release cable.
NOTE
The charge door will automatically lock if not opened within one minute.
Due to freezing weather, it might be difficult to open the charging door. Tap lightly on the door or remove any ice prior to attempting to open. Don’t forcefully open the door.
Opening the Charge Port Door via the Infotainment screen:
Tap  App Library    then  EV app    OR Tap  Battery  icon at the top of Car Control Area.
Go to  Battery   > Unlock Charge Door
Press on the charge port door to open it.
Charge Port Light
This light indicates the vehicle’s charging status as follows:
No charging cable connected: Light OFF.
Charging cable connected, authenticating: Light OFF.
Charging in progress: Light BLINKING.
Charging complete: Light ON.
Charge Port Door - Emergency Release:
In case of an electrical malfunction, the charge port door can be released by pulling on the emergency release cable   located on the left side within the front cargo compartment.
To plug in a charging handle
To insert the charging handle, grasp the cable or plug firmly and push into the charging port until you hear a click. Make sure the connector is fully inserted and flush with the charge port. A poorly inserted connector may cause prolonged charge sessions or faults.
If you are using the Mobile Charger, plug it into the power outlet before plugging it into the charging port on the vehicle. Align the charging handle to the charge port and insert fully. A locking pin will hold the charging handle in place and charging will begin automatically.
After plugging in the charging handle:
The system will start to authenticate the charging station.
After successful authentication, the vehicle will start charging.
NOTE
If the battery temperature is high, you may notice a delay before charging begins as the battery cools down.
The charging handle needs to be inserted into the charging port within 1 minute of opening the charging port. Otherwise the charging port will become inactive. If this occurs, close and re-open the charging port to reactivate it.
If the vehicle has turned off while the charge port door is open, close and the reopen the charge port door to enable charging.
If the charging handle is not fully inserted the locking pin may not engage and charging will not commence.
Refer to the Charging Fault section below for more information.
WARNING
There should be no water, dust, dirt or debris in the charging handle connector and plug. Always make sure they are clean before connecting to the charger and charging inlet. Connecting while there is water, dust, dirt or debris in the charging handle and plug may cause a fire or electric shock.
Be careful not to touch or insert anything in the charging outlet holes, charging port holes, and the charging handle holes when connecting to the charging port in the vehicle.
Immediately stop charging if abnormal symptoms are detected such as an odor or smoke.
If the cable coating is damaged then immediately replace the charging cable to prevent electrical shock.
CAUTION
Make sure the charging connector and charging plug are always clean and dry. Keep the charging cable away from moisture or water.
EV Charging Settings
For details on available EV Charge settings, See >   EV Battery App > EV Settings
Vehicle Charging Status
The following types of charging status will be shown on touchscreen after plugging in the charger:

Battery Status  : Current battery state of charge (%).
Expected Range   : Expected range as per the battery’s current state of charge.
Battery Temperature  .

Battery state of charge   .

NOTE
Energy consumptione by using heating/air conditioning, heated/ventilated seats, defroster, and other functions will consume part of the energy provided by the charger. Reducing usage of these vehicle functions will divert more energy to charging the battery.
Charging values are approximate and for information only.
Cooling system may be activated while charging, so you may hear the air conditioner running.
To stop charging from the touchscreen:
Tap on  Stop Charging   .
Confirm by tapping  Stop Now .
A timer will countdown from 10s to 0s then driver can detach the charge handle from the charge port.
Charging will be stopped.
NOTE
In order to prevent anyone from unplugging the charging handle without authorization, the vehicle must be unlocked before the charging handle can be removed.
During charging, it is normal to hear sounds from the vehicle cooling system, including pumps and fans. Particularly when there is high ambient temperature and high charging current, as the system cools the battery during charging.
Air Conditioning performance may reduce in some circumstances during charging, particularly during high ambient temperature and high charging current. This is normal, to ensure the battery cooling is optimized to maintain battery longevity.
WARNING
When connecting or removing the charging handle, hold the charging handle, not the cable. Pulling the cable itself (without using the handle) may cause the internal wires to break or get disconnected. This may cause electrical shock or fire.
During the charging process, the vehicle will automatically lock the charging handle. Do not use strong force to pull out a locked charging handle, as this may cause serious injury, death or damage to the vehicle.
Manual Release of the Charging handle:
The charging handle is automatically locked in the charge port by a locking pin to prevent accidental or unauthorized disconnection. If the usual methods for releasing the charging handle from the charge port (using the Stop Charging release button) do not work, carefully follow these steps:
Ensure that the vehicle has stopped charging by checking the charging screen on the touchscreen. If necessary, tap on  Stop   Charging .
Turn Off the Vehicle and Remove the Key from the Vehicle.
Open the front cargo compartment.
Remove the Brake Fluid Reservoir access port cover  .
Using a tool, pull the charge port’s release cable   to release the pin that locks the charging handle.
Pull the charging handle from the charge port.
WARNING
Use a screwdriver, hook, pliers or appropriate tool to pull the release cable.
Ensure the vehicle is turned off, as contact with the Front Wiper Motor mechanism could cause serious injury, if the front wiper was turned on.
To close the Charge Port Door:
Close the charge port door and press   to lock it.
CAUTION
Ensure the charging port caps are correctly replaced before closing the charging port door.
Ensure the charging handle and cable are put away correctly, so that they do not cause a tripping hazard, or get caught in or damaged by the vehicle.
Do not drop the charging connector (charging handle). The charging connector can be damaged.
While your vehicle is charging, do not remove the wall plug or mobile charger control box from the wall. Doing so may cause the outlet and cord to be damaged.
AC Scheduled Charging
You can schedule charging by:
Departure time : The charging system will ensure the vehicle is charged to it’s set limit prior to a predefined departure time.
Off-peak hours : The charging system will charge the vehicle at times of off peak power to reduce energy costs and to avoid electrical overload.
See >   EV Battery App  for more information on how to setup scheduled charging.
Charging Faults
Charging Authentication Fault
An authentication fault may occur following connection of the charging handle to the vehicle. An authentication fault may occur when:
The charge station is not compatible with the vehicle
The charging handle or charge station is damaged or not ready to charge
If an authentication fault occurs a message will be shown on the Infotainment Touchscreen. Disconnecting and reconnecting the charging handle or using an alternate charger may correct the authentication issue. Follow all messages shown on the Touchscreen.
If charging does not commence once the charging handle is connected to the vehicle, follow the process below.
Remove the Charging Handle from the Charge Port.
Close the Charge Port Door
Ensure the vehicle is OFF
Lock the Vehicle
Wait 5 minutes for the vehicle to enter Sleep Mode.
Unlock the Vehicle
Open the Charge Port Door
Reconnect the Charging Handle
Check Charging Status
If the issue persists please contact a VinFast Service Center.
Charging handle Fails to Release
If the charging handle can not be removed from the charge port after charging the charging handle locking pin may not have released. If the connector fails to release a message will be shown on the touchscreen and instructions will be provided to manually release the locking pin. For more information See >   Manual Release of the Charg ing handle , earlier in this section.
Battery Leasing Faults (if available)
If an issue exists with a users Battery Leasing account then vehicle charge limits may be reduced until outstanding payments are received. Outstanding invoices can be viewed and actioned via the VinFast app. For further information contact VinFast Customer Support.

## Battery Information

Battery Information
The vehicle has a High Voltage (HV) battery mounted under the vehicle floor and a 12V battery (LV) located at the front of the vehicle.﻿ If the 12V or HV battery needs to be serviced, contact your nearest VinFast Service Center.
For continued optimum battery performance it is important to take proper care of the battery system. For more info on HV and LV batteries see >   Battery Information.
NOTE
When vehicle is left idle and unplugged, it uses energy from the battery for system tests and recharging the 12V battery when necessary.
CAUTION
If the High Voltage battery charging level is less than 5% (Red battery icon in status bar) please charge the vehicle immediately.
If the Battery’s state of charge level falls to 0%, the battery may degrade more quickly.
Battery Leasing Plan Management (if available)
Information regarding the battery lease plan (if applicable) can be viewed on the VinFast Companion App, or by visiting the VinFast Auto website or the nearest VinFast Service Center.

After verification and temporary removal of system restrictions, please move the vehicle to a convenient location to reconnect to the server system. If the vehicle remains restricted and cannot connect to the server, please contact VinFast’s customer care center or service workshop for assistance.
Battery Care
The HV battery is maintenance free. Always pay attention to the battery status and never allow it to fully discharge. Even when the vehicle is not being driven, the battery discharges very slowly to power the on board electronics. On average, the battery discharges at a rate of approximately 1% per day. The discharge rate may vary depending on environmental factors (such as cold weather), configuration, and selected settings. If the vehicle is not used for long periods of time (approx. 2-3 weeks), ensure that the vehicle’s HV battery is always above 30% (preferable between 30% to 60%).
If the high voltage battery needs to be inspected, tested, or replaced, it should only be done by a trained technician. If the 12-volt or high voltage battery needs to be serviced, contact your nearest VinFast Service Center. They will be able to provide information about how the high voltage battery can be recycled.
Operational factors that may affect battery lifespan
During vehicle operation, certain driving habits may subject the high-voltage battery to elevated temperatures and high electrical loads for extended periods, which may affect battery life and performance.
Frequent sudden acceleration
Repeated hard acceleration or aggressive throttle input can cause the battery to deliver high current over a short period, increasing battery temperature and stress on the battery cells.
Recommendation: Accelerate gradually and drive smoothly to help maintain stable battery operation and extend battery life.
Parking in High-Temperature Environments for Extended Periods
High ambient temperatures, especially when the vehicle is parked under direct sunlight for extended periods, can increase battery temperature and accelerate battery aging
Recommendation: Whenever possible, park the vehicle in a shaded, well-ventilated area to help protect the battery.
Operating the Vehicle with the Battery at High Temperature for Extended Periods
Continuous operation at elevated battery temperatures can accelerate side reactions inside the battery cells, reducing battery lifespan.
Recommendation: Reduce aggressive acceleration, drive gently, and allow sufficient time for the battery to cool down when necessary.
NOTE
It is recommended to leave the vehicle plugged in whenever not in use, battery’s lifetime performance increases whenever charged regularly.
For optimal lifetime performance of the battery, minimize the use of Super fast DC Charging
If a battery pack has been involved in a fire it will need to be replaced. It may also need to be returned to the manufacturer for investigation.
If vehicle is unresponsive and does not unlock, open, or charge, the 12V battery may be discharged. In this situation, try jump starting the 12V Battery. If the vehicle is still unresponsive, contact VinFast Service Center.
Charge the vehicle HV battery to 100% SOC at least every month. For any concerns, please contact a VinFast Service Center.
CAUTION
If battery charging level is less than 5% (Red bar in display) please charge the vehicle immediately.
If the Battery’s state of charge level falls to 0%, the battery may degrade more quickly.
Do not store the vehicle near heat sources, which may damage the battery or vehicle.
Do not store the vehicle in areas which may flood, as flooding may damage the battery or vehicle.
WARNING
In case of damage to the high voltage battery or other high voltage components, there is a risk of electric shock, overheating, or fire.
The vehicle should be inspected as soon as possible if it has been damaged from a moderate to severe crash, flood, fire, or other incident. The vehicle should be kept outside until it has been inspected at a distance of at least 15m (50ft) from any structure or anything that can burn. Open a window or door of the vehicle to allow air to circulate inside.
Temperature Limits
Avoid exposing the vehicle to ambient temperatures above 55°C (151°F) or below -35°C (-31°F) for more than 24 hours. Ideally, store the vehicle in climate control garage if extreme ambient temperatures are expected. Failure to follow this temperature recommendation may permanently degrade battery performance.
NOTE
Temperatures below -35°C (-31°F) or above 55°C (151°F) cause the battery to go into protection mode which prevents charging and some features from functioning.
Low Power Consumption Mode
To protect against complete HV battery discharge, the vehicle enters a low power consumption mode when the charge level is very low. In this mode, the HV Battery stops supporting the on-board electronics to slow the discharge rate. Once this low power consumption mode is active it is important to charge the vehicle as soon as possible, to avoid possible battery damage.
When the low-power consumption mode is active, the 12V battery is no longer charged and can completely discharge. If this occurs, the 12V battery will need to be charged or replaced before commencing any HV charging of the vehicle. To conserve energy, select the ECO driving mode from CarBuddy controls.
CAUTION
Do not allow the HV battery to completely discharge. There is a possibility that the HV will no longer charge if left discharged for long periods of time.

# Maintenance


## General Information

Vehicle Care Overview
To ensure trouble free use of the vehicle the maintenance requirements stipulated in this chapter should be carried out regularly. If any abnormal sounds, vibrations occur please visit a VinFast Service Center to have the vehicle inspected. Regular maintenance and servicing is recommended to prevent operational problems with the vehicle.
CAUTION
Service procedures must be carried out by an authorized VinFast technician.

## Fluids, Lubricants and Parts

Cabin Air Filter
The air conditioner cabin filter is a common filter element that prevents dust, pollen and other pollutants from entering the vehicle. ﻿To achieve the best filtering effects, please replace the cabin filter within the specified maintenance interval specified by VinFast.
The cabin air filter should be replaced according to the Maintenance Schedule. If the vehicle is operated in severely air polluted cities or on dusty rough roads for a long period, it should be inspected more frequently and replaced sooner.
NOTE
Filter life depends on atmospheric conditions.
It is recommended that Service procedurest be carried out by an authorized VinFast technician.
Filter Replacement
To remove the Cabin Air filter:
Open the glove box and carefully push in the sides of the glove box past the two side clips   to disengage the glove box﻿.
Remove the Air Filter Cover   by depressing and un-clicking the retaining tab on the right side.
Remove the Air Filter  , by pulling it out.
To install the Cabin Air filter:
To install a new air filter, reassemble in the reverse order of removal process.
Coolant
Coolant is used to maintain the operating temperature of certain components in the vehicle.﻿ Weekly checking of the cooling system is recommended. When checking, the vehicle should be parked on flat ground and the cooling system must be cold. Check the condition of the system hoses, ensuring that they are not cracked, swollen or deteriorated.
Use only designated coolant water for electric vehicles, adding other types of water or antifreeze can damage the vehicle.
The coolant change work should be carried out by a qualified VinFast technician.
See >   Technical Specifications > Fluids, Lubricants and Parts Overview  for the recommended coolant to use.
To Top-up the coolant:
Park the vehicle on flat ground and ensure the vehicles motor compartment has cooled down.
Open the front hood to expose the front cargo compartment.
Locate the Coolant reservoir by removing the panel  .
The cap and bottle should now be accessible. Slowly unfasten the filler cap(s) by turning it in an anti-clockwise direction, if there is a “hiss” sound stop turning the cap and wait until the sound stops. Then proceed to remove it.
Refill using Anti-Freeze Pre-Mixed Coolant AF2100 (ASTM D3306) 50% Glycol & 50% Water mixture.
Fasten and secure the filler cap(s) back on by turning it in a clockwise direction.
NOTE
If the coolant level in the reservoir reaches below 25% of its capacity an audible alert will sound indicating low coolant level.
Ensure the cap and bottle are free from debris and clean before opening to prevent any debris from entering the reservoir.
In an emergency, a small amount of water can be added to the coolant storage tank. However, it should be noted that this will weaken the anti-freeze and anti-corrosion protection and reduces the service life of the coolant. Do not use other components of antifreeze to add to the cooling system.
As the vehicle coolant temperature decreases, the radiator fan will automatically shut off. This is a normal condition.
CAUTION
Keep coolant in a sealed container in a safe place.
Don’t fill the coolant reservoir to a level that it overflows. Coolant left on a metallic surface for long periods of time may cause rust.
WARNING
The coolant reservoir cap should not be removed when the vehicle is ON or when the cooling system is hot. Let the cooling system completely cool before removing the cap. Remove the coolant reservoir cap slowly and cover it with a thick cloth to prevent scalding. Not following these instructions may result in serious injury.
Coolant is extremely toxic and can be fatal if swallowed. Keep coolant tanks sealed and out of reach of children. If a child has accidentally ingestion the coolant, seek medical attention immediately.
Prevent coolant from making contact with skin and eyes. If it does, rinse immediately with plenty of water.
When changing coolant be careful, as there are High Voltage components in this area.
Do not touch heater, radiator, A/C pipes or hoses. They can be very hot and can cause severe burns.
Do not run the vehicle if there is a leak; all coolant could leak out, causing a fire or lead to severe burns. Any leaks must be fixed by an Authorized VinFast Service Center before driving the vehicle.
Keep hands, clothing and tools away from the rotating fan blades of the radiator cooling fan, to avoid injury.
Washer Fluid
Washer fluid is used for cleaning the windshield with the operation of the windshield wipers. It is recommended to always check the fluid level in the washer fluid reservoir and add fluid if necessary.
When the washer fluid level is low, the Low washer fluid symbol   will be displayed on the touchscreen to alert the driver.
The washer fluid reservoir is located under the front hood of the vehicle.
To Top-up the Washer Fluid﻿:
Pull open the washer fluid cap.
Refill washer bottle with washer fluid until full.
Push close the washer bottle cap, ensuring it is properly attached.
NOTE
Always add appropriate washer fluid to the water, if using plain water instead of appropriate washer fluid, anti-freezing agent should be added to the water so that it does not freeze.
Always read the manufacturer’s instructions before using any washer fluid.
CAUTION
Add washer fluid carefully, do not let it splash onto the exterior surface of the vehicle.
The windscreen and wiper may get scratched or damaged if the wiper is turned on without using the washer when the windscreen is dry.
The concentration of anti-freezing agent must be adjusted to the vehicle operating conditions and the climate. A concentration that is too high can lead to vehicle damage.
Adding the appropriate washer fluid when refilling the washer fluid reservoir.
WARNING
Do not touch any High Voltage components in washer fluid reservoir area.
Washer fluid can irritate eyes and skin. Read and observe the instructions provided by the washer fluid manufacturer.
In temperatures below 4° C (40° F), use a washer fluid with antifreeze. In cold weather, using a washer fluid without antifreeze can impair visibility through the windshield.
Do not drink washer fluid and avoid contact with skin. Washer fluid is harmful to humans and animals.
Keep washer fluid away from children and animals.
Wiper Blades
Windshield wiper blades should be inspected on a regular basis for wear or cracking. Contamination of either the windshield or the wiper blades with foreign matter can reduce the effectiveness of the windshield wipers.
Common sources of contamination are:
Insects
Tree sap
Hot wax treatments used by some commercial car washes
If the blades are not wiping properly, clean both the windshield and the blades with a mild detergent, and rinse thoroughly with clean water.
Wiper blades will degrade over time and eventually require replacement. If water is not sufficiently cleared from the windscreen and blades squeak when operating the wipers, blade replacement may be required.
Front Wiper Blades Replacement
To remove the front wiper blades:
Keep the bonnet closed.
The vehicle needs to be in ACC power mode.
Set Wiper Service Mode to On
The front wipers will stop at a 45 degree angle, allowing the owner to lift the wiper arm from the windshield.
Lift the wiper arm away from the windshield.
Open the cover in the direction shown, and pull the upper end of the wiper outwards to release it from the wiper arm.
Remove the wiper from the wiper arm.
Place the joint of the new wiper into the slot of the wiper arm.
Push the wiper towards the wiper arm until the wiper is fully embedded, then close the cover.
Place the wiper assembly back on the windshield and check that the wiper is properly attached to the wiper arm.
Rear Wiper Blade Replacement
The vehicle needs to be in ACC power mode.
Set Wiper Service Mode to On.
The rear wiper will stop in a vertical position, allowing the owner to lift the wiper arm from the windshield﻿.
Replace the rubber wiper blade.
Place the wiper assembly back on the windshield and check that the wiper is properly attached to the wiper arm.
Select Off from the touchscreen to return the Wiper back to the home position.﻿
NOTE
Always use replacement blades that are identical to original blades. Using inappropriate blades can damage the wiper system and windshield.
Allowing the wiper arm to touch the windshield when no wiper blade is installed could damage the windshield. Any damage that occurs would not be covered by the vehicle warranty. Do not allow the wiper arm to touch the windshield.
CAUTION
Wiper blades cannot be locked into a lifted position. Do not lift a wiper arm beyond its intended position, it can cause damage to wiper blade.
Brake Pads & Fluids
Brake pads and brake fluid are the main components of the vehicle braking system, If they get damaged then it will affect the braking ability of the vehicle. The driver should always take care and inspect brake pads and fluids on a regular basis.
Brake Pads
All brake pads should be checked and renewed as necessary. Brake pad wear depends largely on the way the vehicle is driven and on the vehicle operating conditions. Brake pad wear increases when driving frequently in the city or short distances or when driving aggressively.
The vehicle is equipped with brake pad wear indicators. A wear indicator is a thin metal strip that is attached to the brake pad that will produce a squealing sound when it comes in contact with the brake rotor. The sound will be heard when the brake pad material has worn to the end of its service life. If this occurs or there is difficulty stopping the vehicle when the brake pedal is pressed, please contact VinFast Service Center to have the braking system inspected.
NOTE
Always replace brake pads as a set, either both front or both rear sets.
To avoid costly brake repairs, do not continue to drive with worn brake pads.
WARNING
Neglecting to service the vehicle braking system may result in reduced brake system performance, increasing the stopping distance which may lead to a collision.
Replace worn brake pads immediately. Damage to the braking system can result in a braking hazard.
The braking effect may get reduced if the brake pads become wet, after heavy rainfall or after washing the vehicle. Be prepared to exert higher force on the brake pedal in order to compensate.
A salt layer on the brake pads may effect the braking performance of the vehicle.
Overheated brakes reduce the braking effect and considerably increase the braking distance.
Brake Fluid
Brake fluid is a type of hydraulic fluid used in hydraulic brake systems to transfer force into pressure.
The brake fluid level in the reservoir should be checked regularly. Brake fluid will absorb moisture from the atmosphere over time. The hydraulic braking system may get damaged if there is too much moisture in the braking fluid.
To Check the Brake Fluid:
Make sure the vehicle is parked on a flat ground
Let the braking system cool down.
Brake fluid reservoir is located under the cover plate  . Open the cover plate by pulling the plastic tabs on the side of the plate.
The fluid level should be between the MIN and MAX indicator marks as shown on the side of the reservoir.
There are two reasons why the brake fluid level in the reservoir may go down:
Brake pads normal wear. When new pads are installed, the fluid level goes back up.
Fluid leak in the hydraulic brake system. Have the hydraulic brake system fixed. With a leak, the brakes will not work well.
Always clean the brake fluid reservoir cap and the area around the cap before removing and installing the cap.
Do not top off the brake fluid.
If fluid is added due to worn pads, when new brake pads are installed there will be too much fluid. Add or remove fluid, as necessary, only when work is performed on hydraulic brake system.
NOTE
The brake fluid used in this vehicle is DOT 4. Do not mix other types of brake fluid, otherwise it will cause damage to the braking system and components.
The red brake indicator   on the touchscreen will illuminate if the brake fluid in the reservoir drops below the recommended level. The ABS indicator   may also illuminate while driving. Visit a VinFast Service Center as soon as possible.
NOTE
The brake fluid level drops slightly during vehicle operation and as the brake pads wear.
CAUTION
Only use new fluid from a sealed airtight container. Never use previously used fluid or fluid from an opened container, as brake fluid has a tendency to absorb moisture which decreases braking performance.
Do not allow any brake fluid to come into contact with the vehicle paint to avoid corrosion.
Contact VinFast immediately if any increased movement of the brake pedal or a significant loss of brake fluid is present. Driving under these conditions can result in extended stopping distances or complete brake failure.
WARNING
The brake fluid level must be between MIN and MAX level. Brake fluid may leak out over the edge of the reservoir if the brake fluid level is above the MAX marking and it can result in damage to the vehicle.
If the brake light illuminates while driving, stop as soon as safely possible and check the brake fluid. If left unchecked, this may prevent effective braking and cause increased movement in brake pedal and/or complete brake failure.
Brake fluid is highly toxic. Keep brake fluid storage tanks sealed and out of reach of children. Seek medical attention immediately is accidentally exposed to any brake fluid.
Prevent brake fluid from making contact with skin and eyes. If contact is made, rinse immediately with water. If redness, pain or discomfort persists, seek medical attention as soon as possible.
Never touch any of the High Voltage components in the brake fluid reservoir area.
Using the incorrect or contaminated brake fluid could result in damage to the brake system. This could result in the loss of braking leading to a possible injury. Always use the proper manufactured approved brake fluid.
Air Conditioner
In order to ensure continuous and efficient operation of the air conditioning system, regardless of the weather and time of the year, a few minutes of cooling must be performed once a month.
The Air conditioning system in the vehicle is filled with R134a refrigerant. The technical details of the air conditioning refrigerant can be found on the label inside the hood of the vehicle.﻿ Alternatively See >   Technical specification > Fluids, Lubricants and Parts Overview   in this Owner manual.
NOTE
Use only the correct refrigerant and air-conditioning compressor oil.
In humid climates, the air conditioning may create water droplets inside the vehicle. It is recommended to use the air conditioner with the windows closed.
Electric vehicles run much quieter than a gasoline-powered vehicles, therefore the sound of the A/C compressor may be more noticeable.
WARNING
Air conditioning systems contain high-pressured refrigerants. Air conditioning refrigerant systems should only be serviced by qualified personnel. Opening the air conditioner refrigerant system can result in serious injuries.

## Electrical System

Fuses and Circuit Breakers
The vehicle has fuses and circuit breakers to protect against an electrical system overload. The three main fuse boxes in the vehicle are located in the driver side left-hand panel, in the front cargo compartment under the hood, and on top of the 12V battery. ﻿It is strongly recommended to have electrical fuses changed or inspected by VinFast technician.
Whenever a fuse blows, the element inside is melted or broken. If there is a problem and a fuse needs to be replaced, ensure a fuse of the identical size and rating is used. If the fuse on the replacement blows immediately, please contact the local Authorized VinFast Service Center for maintenance as soon as possible.
CAUTION
A fuse must not be replaced with anything but another fuse with the same rating.
Never try to repair a blown fuse.
Do not use copper wire or aluminum foil as a temporary substitute for the proper fuse. Such substitutes can cause extensive wiring damage, possibly resulting in a fire.
Avoid using a screwdriver or any other metal objects to remove fuses, as they may cause short circuits.
WARNING
Fuses and circuit breakers are marked with their ampere rating. Do not exceed the specified amperage rating when replacing fuses and circuit breakers. The use of an oversized fuse or circuit breaker can result in a vehicle fire causing serious injury or death.
Installing fuses that do not meet VinFast’s original fuse specifications is dangerous. The fuses could fail, resulting in a fire causing vehicle damage and/or death.
Spilling liquid on any electrical component on the vehicle may damage it. Always be very careful when working around electrical components.
IP Fusebox
To access instrument panel fuses:
Turn the vehicle Off.
Make sure all switches are in the Off position.
Open the coin cargo compartment, which is located on the lower left hand side of the driver.
Push the inner sides, while pulling out the coin storage box to expose the fuses.
Refer to the description provided inside the fuse panel cover to place the required fuse.
Remove the required fuse by using the removal tool which is provided in the Engine Bay fuse box.
Check and replace the blown fuse.
Make sure the replaced fuse has the same rating and it fits tightly in its place. If in doubt, please contact a VinFast service technician.
Fuse Details:
Front Cargo Compartment Fusebox
To access Front Cargo Compartment fuses:
Turn the vehicle Off.
Make sure all switches are in the Off position.
Open the front cargo compartment.
Remove the fuse box cover plate by pressing the tab at the bottom of the plate.
Remove the fuse panel cover by pressing the two tabs and pulling upwards.
Refer to the description provided inside the fuse panel cover to place the required fuse.
Remove the blown fuse out by using the removal tool which is provided in the Engine Bay fuse box.
Check and replace the blown fuse.
Make sure the replaced fuse has the same rating and it fits tightly in its place. If in doubt please contact a VinFast service technician.
NOTE
Remember to securely install the fuse panel cover back, if not securely attached water may enter and cause electrical damage.
If a multi-fuse has blown, it is recommended to contact a VinFast service technician.
Front Cargo Compartment Fuse Label:
12v Auxiliary Battery Fuse box:
To access 12v Auxiliary Battery Fuse box:
Turn the vehicle Off.
Make sure all switches are in the Off position.
Open the front cargo compartment.
Remove the battery cover plate by pressing the tab at the bottom of the plate.
Remove the fuse panel cover by pressing the two tabs and pulling upwards.
Refer to the description provided inside the fuse panel cover to place the required fuse.
Remove the blown fuse out by using the removal tool which is provided in the Engine Bay fuse box.
Check and replace the blown fuse.
Make sure the replaced fuse has the same rating and it fits tightly in its place. If in doubt please contact a VinFast service technician.
Fuse Label:
Lights
The vehicle is fitted with Light Emitting Diodes (LED’s) in all interior and exterior lights. These are not serviceable items, therefore it is recommended for any light replacement to contact your Authorized VinFast Service Center.﻿ Replacement lights must be of the same type and size as the original lights.
Exterior Lights
